                                      Volume I



                       Ordered Chiral
                             Bar–Cobar

          Reconstruction, Centres, and Collision Geometry


                            Integrated proof manuscript

                                  12 September 2026




This edition develops explicit associative reconstruction, derived collision coefficients,
diagonal centres, complete operations, and ramification. Its proofs do not identify
the internal associative Deligne theorem with the native holomorphic–topological
mixed theorem. The latter comparison is stated with its still unconstructed maps.
The source-to-result register records the extent of the reconstruction; this is not a
certification of every inherited claim.
Preface

A multiplication can be lost while its character remains unchanged. A collision can be lost while
the dimensions of its special fibre remain unchanged. A higher operation can be lost while every
cohomology group remains unchanged. These are not three versions of the same warning. They are
three different calculations, and a reconstruction theory has to explain all of them.
   We begin with a word. Cutting it gives a coalgebra, multiplying adjacent letters gives a differential,
and cutting the resulting words again produces a resolution of the original algebra. The proof is a
contractible two-state complex at each gap. Nothing in that calculation involves a curve. When a
curve enters, its collision coefficients must therefore be constructed separately and retained, rather
than concealed in a new name for the tensor product.
   The resulting geometry is already nontrivial for two charts. Two copies of a line agree off their
origins. Their derived functions have a degree-one principal-part contribution. Their actual derived
special fibre has two idempotents. Replacing the coefficient algebra by its cohomology changes those
idempotents into a nilpotent. With three labels, independent binary orders create two spurious
cyclic branches; a local-cohomology correction removes them. These calculations determine the
coefficient objects used in the geometric chapters.
   The diagonal then supplies a second reconstruction. Over an order-chart base, there are no
additional relative Hochschild directions: the relative centre of the derived coefficient algebra is
the coefficient algebra itself. This statement retains the full derived algebra; it does not make that
algebra formal. A ramified root family behaves differently. Its relative Hochschild cohomology is
entirely even, but its singular fibre has a nonzero Gerstenhaber bracket. The missing information
is an explicit chain homotopy involving the ramification parameter. It obstructs a commutative
refinement of the family centre and invalidates a tempting cohomology-level replacement.
   The book keeps the three bars separate: the transverse associative bar, the associative bar for
an actual chiral tensor, and the chiral Chevalley construction. A relative endomorphism algebra,
an augmentation dual, and a diagonal centre are also separate objects. The internal associative
Deligne action is used with its precise domain. Neither that action nor a coefficient scheme is a
proof of a native mixed Swiss-cheese action on an arbitrary chiral algebra.
   The linear and finite-model arguments are written out. Derived categories, enhanced quasi-
coherent sheaves, the six-functor formalism, and the identification of the brace operad with an E2
operad are identified foundational inputs. Rebuilding those foundations as well would be another
treatise. No calculation in this edition is advertised as a formal verification of all its proofs. The
executable tests check explicitly bounded identities and are accompanied by their bounds.




                                                   i
Conventions and mathematical status

Complexes are cohomologically graded, with V [r]n = V n+r . Thus s : V → V [1] has degree −1. The
differential has degree 1. The tensor convention is

                               (f ⊗ g)(a ⊗ b) = (−1)|g||a| f (a) ⊗ g(b).

Unless another ring is displayed, k has characteristic zero. A claim over an arbitrary ring includes
its flatness or projectivity hypotheses. All derived tensor products and derived Homs use the ring
or monoidal category written beneath them. An unspecified completion is never implicit.
  For an associative algebra A over R, write

                                   ZR (A) = RHomA⊗L Aop (A, A).
                                                       R


This is the relative diagonal centre. It is not RHomA (R, R) unless a separate comparison proves
that assertion. The E2 structure on ZR (A) is the internal associative one. The notation does not
declare a chiral or holomorphic–topological realization.
   Theorem statements in the main text are claims with proofs under their displayed hypotheses. A
paragraph explicitly called a foundational input imports a named theorem. The final comparison
chapter contains problems, not hidden hypotheses for those theorems. The source register is selective
but explicit; unaudited inherited claims are not marked verified.

Chapter 1

Multiplication and the word coalgebra

1.1    A product invisible to a pairing
For λ ∈ k consider the algebra with basis 1, x, z and relations

                                 x2 = λx + z,              xz = zx = z 2 = 0.

It is the quotient k[X]/(X 2 (X − λ)), with x = X and z = X 2 − λX. In particular it is associative.
The augmentation kills x, z. The functional τ (1) = τ (x) = 0, τ (z) = 1 has multiplication pairing
                                                                             
                                            
                                                          0 0 1
                                      τ (ab) a,b=1,x,z = 0 1 0 .
                                                              
                                                          1 0 0

This matrix does not depend on λ. Yet at λ = 0 the algebra is local, with nilpotent maximal
ideal (x), while for λ ̸= 0 the Chinese remainder theorem gives k[X]/X 2 × k. Thus even a fixed
nondegenerate multiplication pairing does not recover the product without the map that uses that
pairing. A character contains still less information.
   The goal of the bar construction is different. It retains every ordered word together with a
differential recording its adjacent products. It does not first compress the product to a scalar.


1.2    Suspended words
Let A be an augmented dg algebra, with augmentation ideal A. A tensor word in sA is written
[a1 | · · · |an ]. Its degree is i |ai | − n. The empty word has degree zero. Set
                                P


                                                                     (sA)⊗n .
                                                                M
                                      BA = T c (sA) =
                                                                n≥0

The direct sum is part of this definition. Its coproduct is deconcatenation:
                                                  n
                                                  X
                           ∆[a1 | · · · |an ] =       [a1 | · · · |ar ] ⊗ [ar+1 | · · · |an ].
                                                  r=0

No permutation is made, so this formula has no additional sign. The graded tensor convention
applies when maps are subsequently evaluated on its two factors.

                                                           1
Chapter 1. Multiplication and the word coalgebra


Lemma 1.1 (Cofree conilpotent coalgebra). Let C be a coaugmented conilpotent graded coalgebra
and V a graded vector space. A degree-zero map f : C → V extends uniquely to a coaugmented
coalgebra map F : C → T c (V ). Its length-n component is
                                                               (n−1)
                                              Fn = f ⊗n ∆               ,          n ≥ 1.

Proof. Conilpotence means that for each c the iterated reduced coproduct is zero in all sufficiently
large lengths. Thus the displayed components define an element of the direct sum, not an infinite
formal word. Coassociativity identifies each cut of an iterated coproduct with the tensor product
of the two shorter iterates, proving the coalgebra identity. Conversely, project that identity to
V ⊗r ⊗ V ⊗(n−r) . Induction on n forces the length-n component from the length-one component.
This proves existence and uniqueness without dualizing an infinite-dimensional algebra.

  A coderivation is likewise controlled by its projection to the cogenerators. If qr : V ⊗r → V has
degree d, its extension to a word is the sum of all consecutive insertions,
                                   n−r
                                         (−1)d(|v1 |+···+|vi |) v1 · · · vi qr (vi+1 · · · vi+r ) vi+r+1 · · · vn .
                                   X
               q(v1 · · · vn ) =
                                   i=0

The formula follows by applying the coderivation identity at every cut. It also proves uniqueness: a
coderivation with zero cogenerator projection has zero projection to each positive word length, by
induction. The same statement holds for a coderivation along a fixed coalgebra map.


1.3     The differential and its signs
Define the two cogenerator maps

                            b1 (sa) = −s(dA a),                 b2 (sa ⊗ sb) = (−1)|a| s(ab).

Both have degree one. Extend them as coderivations and set b = b1 + b2 . On a word,
                                               n             P
                                               X          1+           (|aj |−1)
                      b1 [a1 | · · · |an ] =       (−1)          j<i               [a1 | · · · |dA ai | · · · |an ],
                                               i=1
                                               n−1        P
                                               X                     |aj |−i+1
                      b2 [a1 | · · · |an ] =         (−1)      j≤i               [a1 | · · · |ai ai+1 | · · · |an ].
                                               i=1

For degree-zero letters the second line has alternating signs +, −, +, . . ..

Theorem 1.2 (The tensor bar is a dg coalgebra). The coderivation b squares to zero. The length
filtration is exhaustive and conilpotent, and A 7→ BA is functorial for augmented dg algebra maps.

Proof. The square of a degree-one coderivation is a coderivation. Its cogenerator projection can
only receive contributions from words of lengths one, two and three. In length one it is sd2A a = 0. In
length two the mixed terms are precisely the difference between dA (ab) and (dA a)b + (−1)|a| a(dA b),
with a common suspension sign. They vanish by the derivation identity.
   For clarity, in length three the first multiplication contributes

                                    (−1)|a|+|a|+|b| s((ab)c) = (−1)|b| s((ab)c).

                                                                 2
Chapter 1. Multiplication and the word coalgebra


Multiplication of the final pair first has the coderivation sign (−1)|a|−1 and the binary sign (−1)|b| ;
the second multiplication then has sign (−1)|a| . Their product is (−1)|b|−1 . The total is therefore

                                     (−1)|b| s((ab)c − a(bc)) = 0.

Uniqueness of coderivations proves the square-zero identity on all words. The reduced coproduct
decreases the number of letters in each nonempty factor; its nth iterate vanishes on words of length
at most n. Finally an augmented dg algebra map preserves b1 , b2 on cogenerators and preserves all
cuts, proving functoriality.

  The proof identifies the hypotheses actually used: d2A = 0, the derivation identity, and associativity.
A Lie bracket supplies Jacobi instead of the last identity and does not define this differential. In
particular a vertex-algebra normally ordered product cannot be substituted unless its associativity
has been established on the chosen carrier.
Calculation 1.3 (Four letters). For ungraded letters,

                               b[a|b|c|d] = [ab|c|d] − [a|bc|d] + [a|b|cd].

Applying b again gives
                                 [(ab)c − a(bc)|d] − [a|(bc)d − b(cd)].
Disjoint adjacent multiplications cancel before associativity is used. The remaining two terms are
exactly the local three-letter associators. This is the elementary mechanism behind the general
coderivation proof.


1.4     The unnormalized module resolution
An augmentation is not required to resolve a module. Let M be a left module over an ordinary
unital R-algebra A. The simplicial bar resolution has

                                       Pn = A ⊗R A⊗R n ⊗R M,

with faces multiplying adjacent algebra factors and the final face applying the module action. Its
differential is ∂ = ni=0 (−1)i di . The augmented underlying R-complex has extra degeneracy
                   P


                  h(a0 ⊗ · · · ⊗ an ⊗ m) = 1 ⊗ a0 ⊗ · · · ⊗ an ⊗ m,     h(m) = 1 ⊗ m.

The identities d0 h = 1 and di h = hdi−1 for i > 0 give ∂h + h∂ = 1. This is an R-linear contraction,
not an A-linear one, which is exactly what is required to establish exactness.
   Over a field the terms are free A-modules, so P• → M is a projective resolution. Over a
general ring the same conclusion requires the displayed free factors to be projective over R, or a
projective/cofibrant replacement. Exactness of the underlying augmented bar does not by itself
prove projectivity. With dg inputs, one may assign total degree q − n to internal degree q and
simplicial degree n, and use dint + (−1)q ∂. The faces commute with the internal differential, so
these two total terms anticommute.
  For an augmented algebra over a field, quotienting degeneracies gives the normalized bar resolution
A ⊗ BA. Tensoring it with the trivial right module yields BA. Thus the bar computes TorA (k, k).
Applying Hom to the module resolution computes derived endomorphisms of that module. The
module has to be named: a trace is not a module structure on the scalar ring.

                                                    3
Chapter 1. Multiplication and the word coalgebra


1.5     Coalgebra, algebra, and topology
For finite-dimensional V , the full ungraded dual of n V ⊗n is n (V ∨ )⊗n . It is an algebra under dual
                                                   L           Q

deconcatenation. The degreewise dual in graded complexes need not be the same ungraded vector
space. For A = k[ϵ]/ϵ2 , |ϵ| = 0, the word of length n is in degree −n, so each fixed cohomological
degree contains just one line. Its graded dual is the graded tensor algebra on a degree-one generator.
Forgetting degrees first produces the completed algebra instead.
   Every fixed finite word window has ordinary finite-dimensional duality. The discrepancy concerns
the assembly of infinitely many windows, not a false duality in every individual positive weight.
The continuous dual of a completed product returns a direct sum when the topology is the product
topology and the target is discrete. One must name this topology before using a continuous pairing.
   Finally, completion changes conilpotence. In the square-zero algebra with generator x of positive
weight, the formal series n≥1 [x| · · · |x] belongs to the weight-completed bar but has no finite
                           P

coradical degree. A complete coalgebra may be pro-conilpotent in its specified tower without being
an ordinary conilpotent coalgebra. This distinction will recur in every reconstruction statement.

2.1     Twisting cochains
Let C = k ⊕ C be a coaugmented dg coalgebra. Put t = s−1 and define
                                                                         ′
                                                                 (−1)|c | (tc′ )(tc′′ ),
                                                               X
                      ΩC = T (tC),        d(tc) = −t(dC c) −

                 c′ ⊗ c′′ . Extend this as a derivation. The internal square vanishes by d2C = 0;
              P
where ∆c =
the mixed terms vanish because dC is a coderivation; and the two triple-word terms cancel by
coassociativity. Hence d2 = 0.
  A twisting cochain τ : C → A has degree one, vanishes on the coaugmentation, has image in A,
and satisfies
                                dA τ + τ dC + µ(τ ⊗ τ )∆ = 0.                            (2.1)
                               ′
Here τ ⊗ τ contributes (−1)|c | on c′ ⊗ c′′ .

Proposition 2.1 (Classical adjunction). For conilpotent C there are natural bijections

                        HomdgAlg (ΩC, A) ∼
                                         = Tw(C, A) ∼
                                                    = HomdgCoalg (C, BA).

The free tensor cobar is left adjoint to the tensor bar.

Proof. An algebra map out of T (tC) is determined on its generators, equivalently by τ . Commutation
with differentials on tc is exactly (2.1). A coalgebra map to T c (sA) is determined by sτ , by Lemma 1.1.
The cogenerator projection of the chain-map defect is the negative suspension of (2.1). Its vanishing
makes the entire defect zero by uniqueness for coderivations along a coalgebra map. Conilpotence
ensures the extension is finite on each element. These constructions are natural in both variables.

  The counit ΩBA → A takes t[a] to a and every generator t[a1 | · · · |an ] with n > 1 to zero. For
ungraded a, b,
                               d(t[a|b]) = −t[ab] + t[a]t[b],
whose image is zero. This elementary check fixes both signs in the cobar formula. The unit C → BΩC
has first component c 7→ [tc] and higher components given by iterated reduced coproducts.

                                                    5
Chapter 2. Reconstruction by the complexes of cuts


2.2     An exhaustive-filtration lemma
Lemma 2.2. Suppose K has an increasing filtration by subcomplexes 0 = F−1 K ⊂ F0 K ⊂ · · ·
with union K. If every Fn K/Fn−1 K is acyclic, then K is acyclic. A filtered map inducing quasi-
isomorphisms on all associated-graded pieces is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof. A cycle x lies in some Fn K. Its associated-graded class is a boundary; subtract a differential
in Fn K to put it in Fn−1 K. Repeating at most n + 1 times expresses x as a boundary. Apply this
argument to the filtered mapping cone for the second assertion. No boundedness in cohomological
degree has been used.

   For a decreasing filtration the same procedure can require an infinite sum. It is not valid unless
that sum exists and separation and convergence are controlled. The word filtrations below are
chosen so that this issue is either absent or finite in a fixed positive weight.


2.3     The counit cut complex
A word in ΩBA is a word of nonempty words. At each gap between the original A-letters there are
two states: the gap is inside a bar word, or it separates two cobar factors. Write these states as
                               K −1 = kα,          K 0 = kβ,               dα = β.
The contraction is h(β) = α, h(α) = 0.
Theorem 2.3 (Unconditional algebraic counit). For every augmented dg algebra over a field, the
algebraic direct-sum counit
                                       ΩBA −→ A
is a quasi-isomorphism. No quadratic presentation or positive auxiliary weight is required.

Proof. Filter by the number n of original A-letters. The internal differential preserves n, bar
multiplication lowers it, and cobar splitting preserves it. The filtration is increasing, exhaustive and
bounded below. At n ≥ 1 its associated graded is the interleaved tensor complex
                                     A ⊗ K ⊗ A ⊗ · · · ⊗ K ⊗ A,
with n letter factors and n − 1 gaps.
   We check the splitting sign, rather than identify only the underlying vector spaces. At the gap
following letter j, let cj be the number of outside gaps before it. The cobar formula gives sign
                                                P
                                              1+          |ai |−j+cj
                                         (−1)       i≤j                .
The degree preceding that gap in the interleaved complex is i≤j |ai | − (j − 1 − cj ). These exponents
                                                                       P

differ by an even integer. The internal letter signs agree because every preceding inside gap lowers
the degree by one. Thus the identification is one of complexes.
    For n ≥ 2, contract the first gap, multiplying the contraction by (−1)|a1 | when it passes the first
letter. Differential terms on other factors cancel with the opposite order of this odd homotopy. The
first gap gives the identity. At n = 1 the complex is A, and at n = 0 it is k. Give A filtration
F0 A = k, Fn A = A for n ≥ 1. The counit is the identity on the first two associated-graded pieces
and maps the other contractible pieces to zero. Lemma 2.2 proves the result.

                                                     6
Chapter 2. Reconstruction by the complexes of cuts


   For three ungraded letters the four gap states form a square:

                                     t[a|b|c] 7→ t[a]t[b|c] − t[a|b]t[c],
                                   t[a]t[b|c] 7→ t[a]t[b]t[c],
                                   t[a|b]t[c] 7→ t[a]t[b]t[c].

The two paths cancel. The terms omitted in this square are precisely the terms multiplying original
letters, which lie in the preceding filtration stage.


2.4     The unit cut complex and positive weights
Suppose C = k ⊕ w>0 Cw , with differential preserving positive integral weight and reduced
                     L

coproduct adding it. In total weight w a word has at most w original letters. For BΩC, an inside
gap is now within a cobar word, of degree one, and an outside gap is a bar separation, of degree
zero. Use
                              L0 = kβ,     L1 = kα,     dβ = −α.
Theorem 2.4 (Positive-weight reconstruction). The unit C → BΩC is a quasi-isomorphism in every
weight. Bar and cobar induce inverse equivalences after inverting weightwise quasi-isomorphisms of
positive-weight augmented algebras and coaugmented coalgebras. The same is true of their products
over weight with tensor product completed in that same weight.

Proof. In fixed weight filter BΩC decreasingly by the number of original C-letters. Coproduct
terms increase it and outer bar multiplication preserves it. The filtration has only w positive stages.
The associated graded is the interleaved complex with L at the gaps. Its n ≥ 2 pieces contract at
the first gap; its n = 1 piece is C. The leading unit component is the identity on that piece. Finite
filtration proves the unit assertion.
   Bar preserves quasi-isomorphisms over a field by its increasing length filtration and tensor
exactness. Cobar preserves weightwise quasi-isomorphisms by the finite decreasing length filtration in
each weight. The unit, counit and adjunction identities therefore descend to the specified localizations.
For completion define the weight-w tensor by the finite sum over positive decompositions of w, prove
each assertion there, and take the product over w. Products of vector spaces are exact. Equivalently
take the derived inverse limit of the finite weight truncations. This proves only the stated weight
completion, not a different pole or arity completion.

   The usual unit statement for an ordinary conilpotent coalgebra also follows using its increasing
coradical filtration. The associated graded is positive-weight, the filtration on tensor words is
additive in coradical degrees, and the increasing exhaustive lemma applies. This assertion about the
unit must not be confused with preservation of arbitrary coalgebra quasi-isomorphisms by cobar.


2.5     Why weak equivalences are part of the theorem
Let A = k × k with augmentation the first projection and reduced idempotent e = (0, 1). The
reduced bar has one word en in each degree −n and
                                                  (
                                                      en−1   n even,
                                       b(en ) =
                                                      0      n odd.

                                                       7
Chapter 2. Reconstruction by the complexes of cuts


Thus BA → k is an underlying quasi-isomorphism of coalgebras. But H 0 (ΩBA) = A, by Theorem 2.3,
whereas H 0 (Ωk) = k. Cobar does not carry this coalgebra quasi-isomorphism to a quasi-isomorphism.
   There is no contradiction: an adjunction descends only when its functors preserve the chosen
weak equivalences. The classical coalgebra theory uses cobar-detected equivalences, or additional
hypotheses such as the positive weights above. A higher-categorical bar which is left adjoint to
a totalization cobar belongs to another adjunction. Its completion locus cannot be inserted into
Proposition 2.1 without a comparison theorem.


2.6     The finite tensor-nilpotent form
Let V be a stable linear nonunital monoidal category with exact tensor and suppose all products
of more than N objects vanish. The preceding constructions then have finite arity. For a dg
presentation their cut contractions use only matrices with entries 0, 1, −1, so make sense internally.
For homotopy-coherent associative objects, the operadic bar filtration has the same finite range and
the same cut-complex associated graded. The standard associative operadic construction supplies
its higher coherences; the finite argument proves its reconstruction maps are equivalences.
   This is the associative specialization of the pro-nilpotent operadic framework in [2]. It applies to
the stable coefficient category actually supplied. It does not prove that a nonlinear full subcategory
defined by factorization equivalences is closed under its finite sums or normalized word complexes.
That question has to be settled by a separate preservation argument.

Chapter 3

Higher operations from a contraction

3.1     The first nontrivial identity
For a graded space W , a collection of degree-one maps qn : W ⊗n → W extends to a coderivation of
T c (W ). It squares to zero exactly when, for every n,
                                            qr+1+t (1⊗r ⊗ qs ⊗ 1⊗t ) = 0.
                                    X

                                  r+s+t=n

All tensor evaluation signs are included. On W = sH this is the definition of an augmented A∞
structure. The unsuspended operation has degree 2 − n.
   If q1 = 0, the arity-three equation contains only the two insertions of q2 . It asserts associativity.
A ternary operation cannot repair a nonassociative binary multiplication in a minimal A∞ algebra:
the terms involving q3 in arity three would have to compose with q1 . This simple observation
excludes several proposed identifications of a lambda-bracket associator with a minimal ternary
multiplication. The correct ternary operation is obtained from an actual chain contraction.


3.2     Splitting the complex
Let (W, d) be a complex of vector spaces. Choose representatives H n ⊂ ker d, write B n = im(d :
W n−1 → W n ), and choose a complement Ln to ker d. Then
                                                                     ∼

Let i, p be inclusion and projection for H, and let h be the inverse of d on boundaries, zero on the
other summands. These maps satisfy
                        pi = 1,      dh + hd = 1 − ip,          h2 = hi = ph = 0.
The choices can be made weightwise. Over a general ring, a splitting with these properties may
not exist; the integral examples later show that this is substantive rather than an inconvenience of
notation.
   Write e = ip. On tensor powers define
                                            n

                                            X

Moreover Hn i⊗n = 0 and p⊗n Hn = 0. This formula is the source of the recursive higher operations.


3.3     The recursive construction
Apply the splitting to W = sA with its differential b1 . Let H = H(W ). The product is the
degree-one map b2 from Chapter 1.

Theorem 3.1 (Minimal transfer with explicit defects). There are a minimal coderivation q on
T c (H) and a dg coalgebra map F : (T c (H), q) → (T c (W ), b) with F1 = i. Set q1 = 0, F1 = i.
Recursively define
                                         X
                               En =             b2 (Fa ⊗ Fb )
                                        a+b=n

                                                      Fr+1+t (1⊗r ⊗ qs ⊗ 1⊗t ),
                                              X
                                        −
                                            r+s+t=n
                                            2≤s≤n−1
                                qn = pEn ,         Fn = −hEn .

These formulas define an A∞ quasi-isomorphism and preserve any weights respected by the contraction
and product.

Proof. Suppose the equations hold below arity n and temporarily set qn = Fn = 0. The cogenerator
component of R = bF − F q in arity n is En . Expanding gives

                                              bR + Rq = −F q 2 .

All lower defects vanish. Since q1 = 0, the arity-n cogenerator identity is b1 En = −i(q 2 )n . Apply p:
the left side is zero and pi = 1, so (q 2 )n = 0. Thus b1 En = 0.
   Now b1 (−hEn ) = −En + ipEn , by the contraction identity. After defining Fn , qn as above, the
arity-n defect is zero. The value of (q 2 )n is unchanged, since an occurrence of qn there would have
to compose with q1 . This closes the induction. Every fixed word uses only finitely many arities, so
the collection defines a coalgebra map and coderivation on the direct sum. Its linear part induces
the cohomology isomorphism. Weight preservation follows term by term.

   For example,
                     q2 = pb2 (i, i),       q3 = −pb2 (hb2 (i, i), i) − pb2 (i, hb2 (i, i)).
The two planar binary trees are visible, but their coefficients are fixed by the defect recursion, not
by an informal picture. The terms involving F2 q2 disappear after p because pF2 = 0.

Chapter 3. Higher operations from a contraction


3.4     A reverse map
There is a coalgebra chain map P : (T c (W ), b) → (T c (H), q) with P1 = p and P F = 1. Here is the
recursive construction, which also explains why a chosen transfer is not a new invariant. Suppose
P b − qP and P F − 1 vanish below arity n. With Pn = 0, denote their arity-n cogenerator parts by
En′ , kn . Restoring Pn changes them to

                                     Pn dn + En′ ,        Pn i⊗n + kn .

The square-defect identity gives En′ dn = 0. Composing with F and using bF = F q gives En′ i⊗n = 0.
Hence
                                        Pn = −En′ Hn − kn p⊗n
solves both equations, by the tensor contraction. This constructs P .
   An A∞ map between minimal structures with invertible linear component has a unique coalgebra
inverse. Indeed the arity-n equation for an inverse is triangular: its new component occurs multiplied
by the invertible linear term, and every other term is already known. Left and right inverses agree.
Multiplying the chain-map identity by the inverse shows it is a chain map. Thus two choices of
contraction give isomorphic minimal structures, although their displayed higher coefficients may
differ.


3.5     An essential Massey operation
Consider D = T (u, v), with |u| = |v| = 1, du = 0, dv = u2 . Then

                                     d(uv + vu) = −u3 + u3 = 0.

Its degree-two boundaries are multiples of u2 , so uv + vu is not exact. The degree-one cohomology is
generated by u, whose square is zero. Therefore the triple Massey product ⟨[u], [u], [u]⟩ is represented,
up to the fixed convention, by uv + vu and has zero indeterminacy. It does not contain zero.
    More generally, for closed degree-one a, b, c with ab = dU and bc = dV , the expression U c + aV
is closed. Changing U, V changes its class by

                                       H |a|+|b|−1 c + aH |b|+|c|−1 .

A Massey product is this coset, not one preferred polynomial. A nonzero displayed q3 does not
prove nonformality unless its class survives the permitted changes. Conversely a Massey coset not
containing zero is preserved by quasi-isomorphisms and obstructs formality.


3.6     Obstruction theory with its actual filtration
Let g = F 1 g ⊃ F 2 g ⊃ · · · be a dg Lie algebra with dF r ⊂ F r and [F r , F s ] ⊂ F r+s . Assume
characteristic zero. Put gr = g/F r+1 and Ar = F r /F r+1 .
Theorem 3.2 (One obstruction layer). For ar−1 ∈ MC(gr−1 ), the curvature of any graded lift
e ∈ g1r gives a lift-independent class in H 2 (Ar ). A Maurer–Cartan lift exists exactly when that class
a
vanishes. When it does, lifts modulo gauge transformations trivial below this layer form a torsor
under H 1 (Ar ).

                                                     11
Chapter 3. Higher operations from a contraction


Proof. The curvature da e + 12 [a  e] lies in A2r . The Bianchi identity says its differential is minus its
                                e, a
bracket with a e. That bracket lies in F r+1 , so the curvature is closed in the layer. Replacing the
        e + u changes curvature by du, since all other terms have higher filtration. Thus its class is
lift by a
independent and vanishes precisely when a correction exists. Differences of corrected lifts are closed
degree-one elements. A degree-zero gauge change at this layer adds an exact element, giving the
stated torsor.

    If the complex is separated and complete, compatible Maurer–Cartan representatives in all
quotients determine a unique solution: lift the degree-one elements by completeness and detect
its curvature by separation in degree two. A sequence of unrelated gauge classes does not provide
compatible representatives. Even compatible representatives need not come from an element without
completeness. In tk[t] concentrated in degree one, the classes t + · · · + tr−1 are compatible modulo
tr but no polynomial represents them all.
   Finally, two individual solutions need not combine. For a, b individually Maurer–Cartan, the
curvature of a+b is [a, b]. Degree-one generators x, y with [x, x] = [y, y] = 0 and central [x, y] = z ̸= 0
give a finite counterexample. A mixed operadic theorem has to exhibit the correction to this cross
term in its actual operation complex. Merely writing its Maurer–Cartan equation does not supply
that correction.


3.7     Transfer from an already higher algebra
The preceding construction is not restricted to a strict binary input. Suppose b = b1 + b2 + b3 + · · · is
a degree-one square-zero coderivation of the conilpotent tensor coalgebra, with no nullary coefficient,
and the contraction is for b1 . For each input length n there are only finitely many relevant Taylor
coefficients, even if there are infinitely many in the entire structure.

Theorem 3.3 (General finite-on-words transfer). With F1 = i, q1 = 0, define
               n
                                                                                Fr+1+t (1⊗r ⊗ qs ⊗ 1⊗t ).
               X        X                                           X
        En =                       bk (Fn1 ⊗ · · · ⊗ Fnk ) −
               k=2 n1 +···+nk =n                               r+s+t=n, 2≤s<n

Then qn = pEn and Fn = −hEn construct a minimal higher algebra and a higher quasi-isomorphism
to b in all arities. The signs are those of the tensor evaluation convention.

Proof. At an unfinished length put Fn = qn = 0 and let E = bF − F q. The exact identity bE + Eq =
−F q 2 has cogenerator component b1 En = −i(q 2 )n because all smaller defects are zero. Applying
p gives (q 2 )n = 0, hence b1 En = 0. The contraction identity then gives b1 (−hEn ) = −En + ipEn ,
which kills the length-n defect. Neither insertion of qn into q 2 contributes at this length, since
q1 = 0. This closes the induction. Every sum at a fixed word is finite, so the constructed coefficients
define actual coalgebra maps. The first component is the specified cohomology inclusion. Units are
handled on normalized positive-arity inputs when the contraction preserves the unit, by the same
adjacent-unit cancellations as in the strict case.

Small algebras with different

4.1    Free and square-zero algebras
For a vector space V , compare T (V ) with k ⊕ V , V 2 = 0. The latter has

                                       B(k ⊕ V ) = T c (sV )

with zero multiplication differential. For the free algebra there is a coalgebra quasi-isomorphism

                                k ⊕ sV −→ BT (V ),       sv 7−→ [v].

To prove it, expand every bar entry into original V -letters. At fixed total letter count n, bar
subdivisions are choices of cuts in n − 1 gaps. The differential removes one cut. For n > 1 the
corresponding tensor of two-term identity complexes contracts at the first gap. At n = 1 it is sV .
The inclusion preserves coproducts because its positive generators are primitive.
  The same result follows from the free left-module resolution

                              0 −→ T (V ) ⊗ V −→ T (V ) −→ k −→ 0.

Every nonempty word has a unique last letter, which proves injectivity and identifies the image.
In particular free and square-zero algebras on the same generators have radically different bar
homology. A list of generators is not a substitute for the differential.


4.2    The quantum plane
Fix q ∈ k× and put
                                     Aq = k⟨x, y⟩/(yx − qxy).
Reducing yx to qxy terminates by inversion number. The word yx has no proper suffix equal to a
proper prefix, so there is no overlapping two-rule ambiguity. Disjoint reductions commute. Thus
xa y b is a basis, and
                                    (xa y b )(xc y d ) = q bc xa+c y b+d .


                                                13
Chapter 4. Small algebras with different reconstruction data


The algebra is a domain: the leading normal monomials of two nonzero polynomials multiply with
a nonzero power of q.
   The augmented left resolution is
                                           d                d
                                         2
                           0 −→ Aq (−2) −→ Aq (−1)2 −→
                                                     1
                                                       Aq −→ k −→ 0,

with
                            d1 (u, v) = ux + vy,          d2 (w) = (wy, −qwx).
The composite is the relation. Injectivity of d2 follows from the domain property. For exactness in
the middle, expand u, v in the normal basis. The coefficients of x0 y b in ux + vy = 0 imply that v
has no terms with x-degree zero. Write uniquely v = −qwx, using q ̸= 0. The kernel equation then
becomes (u − wy)x = 0, so u = wy. This proves exactness without inferring it from a Hilbert-series
identity.
   Applying HomAq (−, k) kills the differentials. The Ext dimensions are 1, 2, 1. The quadratic
relation space is spanned by y ⊗ x − qx ⊗ y. Its annihilator in the dual tensor square is spanned by

                                         ξ2,   η2,    ξη + qηξ.

The normalized bar relation cycle [y|x] − q[x|y] identifies the product with this quotient, under the
tensor-dual convention of Chapter 1. Thus the parameter survives in the product even though the
dimensions do not change.


4.3     The Jordan plane and a categorical correction
Let
                                    AJ = k⟨x, y⟩/(yx − xy − x2 ).
Again the only leading word is yx, with no self-overlap, so normal monomials xa y b form a basis.
More explicitly it is the Ore extension k[x][y; δ] with δ(x) = x2 . The filtration by y-degree has
polynomial associated graded, proving it is a domain. The resolution has the same ranks as before
and
                                    d2 (w) = (w(y − x), −wx).
The composite is zero. The second component proves injectivity. Its image equals the kernel degree
by degree: dim(AJ )n = n + 1, the map to the augmentation ideal is onto, and in degree n ≥ 1 the
kernel dimension is 2n − (n + 1) = n − 1, exactly the dimension of the injective degree-n source of
d2 . The degree-zero assertion is immediate. Therefore the resolution is exact.
   The quadratic bar class is
                                         [y|x] − [x|y] − [x|x].
It is not the quantum-plane class. The two examples demonstrate that Betti numbers do not
determine products. They do not, however, prove that the graded module categories are inequivalent.
Proposition 4.1 (An explicit twist). The Jordan plane is a graded twist of k[x, y]. Its graded
right-module category is equivalent to that of the polynomial algebra.

Proof. On S = k[x, y] let σ(x) = x, σ(y) = y − x, and define the product of homogeneous elements
by a ∗ b = aσ deg a (b). Then
                                    y ∗ x − x ∗ y = x2 = x ∗ x.

                                                     14
Chapter 4. Small algebras with different reconstruction data


The generators therefore give a surjective graded map from AJ to S σ . Both sides have dimension
n + 1 in degree n, so the map is an isomorphism.
   For a graded right S-module M , define m ∗ a = mσ deg m (a) on homogeneous elements. Then

                           (m ∗ a) ∗ b = mσ deg m (aσ deg a (b)) = m ∗ (a ∗ b).

Twisting back by σ −1 gives the inverse functor. The underlying graded vector spaces and degree-zero
module maps are unchanged, so the functors are exact and mutually inverse. This proves the
categorical claim directly.

   The equivalence is not an assertion that every additional marking, shift identification, tensor
structure or chosen augmentation-dual multiplication is preserved in a prescribed way. Those data
have to be transported. Nonisomorphic algebras and inequivalent structured presentations are
different assertions.


4.4     Relative quiver words
         L
Let S = i∈Q0 kei and let M be the arrow S-bimodule of a finite quiver. Relative tensor words use
⊗S , which keeps only composable arrows. The free algebra TS (M ) has

                                         S ⊗LTS (M ) S ≃ S ⊕ sM,

by the same last-letter resolution. Semisimplicity of S supplies the needed tensor exactness.
          a    b
   For 1 −
         →2− → 3 with relation ba = 0, the augmentation ideal contains just a, b and has square
zero. The only length-two relative word is [b|a]. There is no length-three word. Consequently

                   Tor0 = S,     Tor1 = ka ⊕ kb,        Tor2 = k[b|a],      Torn≥3 = 0.

For left modules the resolution of the simple at vertex 1 is
                                           ·b      ·a
                               0 −→ Ae3 −
                                        → Ae2 −

The endpoint labels yield Ext1 (S1 , S2 ) = k and Ext2 (S1 , S3 ) = k. Replacing S by one scalar field
would insert noncomposable words and change the calculation.


4.5     No false augmentation
The Weyl algebra W = k⟨p, q⟩/(pq − qp − 1) has no unital scalar augmentation: the scalar images
of p, q commute, contradicting the relation. The same is true of Matn (k) for n > 1, since a unital
map from this simple algebra would have zero kernel and inject it into a commutative field.
   These examples still have modules, diagonal centres and relative resolutions. One may choose a
module and compute its endomorphism algebra, retain the constant term as curvature in a suitable
presentation, or work over a relative idempotent algebra. A trace is not an augmentation: it does
not satisfy τ (ab) = τ (a)τ (b) in these examples.
   A useful control is

              Bn = k[ϵ]/ϵ2 × Matn (k),     ε(a + bϵ, M ) = a,    τ (a + bϵ, M ) = b + Tr M.

                                                   15
Chapter 4. Small algebras with different reconstruction data


The multiplication pairing is nondegenerate and symmetric. The distinguished module lives entirely
in the first product factor, so its augmentation bar has the same homology as the dual numbers.
On the other hand the Frobenius handle element is (2ϵ, nIn ), and its traced powers are

                        τ (1) = n,      τ (e) = 2 + n2 ,     τ (eg ) = ng+1   (g ≥ 2).

These are calculations of a specified trace and element. They are not a proof that a bar homology
group determines a sewing theory.


4.6     A nonhomogeneous free resolution
The dg algebra

               Qκ = (T (p, q, h), d),       |p| = |q| = 0,     |h| = −1,      dh = pq − qp − κ

resolves Wκ = k⟨p, q⟩/(pq − qp − κ). To see this, expand each occurrence of h as a placeholder
pq. Filter first by the expanded length and then by the inversion number of the expanded word.
Replacing h by pq preserves the index; replacing it by qp lowers inversions, and replacing it by κ
lowers length. The associated-graded summand for an expanded word is a tensor product of the
two-term complexes kh → k(pq) at its occurrences of pq. These occurrences are disjoint, because
pq has no self-overlap. If one is present the summand contracts. Otherwise the word is q a pb and
survives in degree zero. The increasing exhaustive-filtration lemma proves the quasi-isomorphism.
   For κ ̸= 0 this resolution is unital but not augmented by killing its generators. Its constant
differential term is genuine information. A homogeneous quadratic approximation that deletes it is
a different algebra, not a smaller quasi-isomorphic presentation of the same one.




                                                      16
Chapter 5

Lie envelopes, parameters, and the
diagonal

5.1     The Jacobi overlap proves PBW
Let g have an ordered basis. Reduce every inverted pair yx by xy + [y, x]. Word length decreases in
the bracket term, and inversion number decreases in the sorted term. Disjoint reductions commute.
The only overlapping ambiguity has three letters z > y > x. The two reductions differ, after
reducing smaller words, by
                                [[y, x], z] + [y, [z, x]] + [[z, y], x] = 0.
This is Jacobi. A minimal word with two distinct normal forms would have first reductions that
agree, commute, or are this overlap; in every case smaller reductions join them. Thus there is no such
word. Nondecreasing monomials form a basis of U (g), and its word filtration has gr U (g) = Sym g.
The proof works for an infinite basis because each individual bracket is a finite linear combination
and each reduction terminates.


5.2     The exterior resolution and its parameter
For an ordinary Lie algebra, use the left resolution with differential
                                        X
            d(u ⊗ x1 ∧ · · · ∧ xn ) =       (−1)i+1 uxi ⊗ x1 ∧ · · · x
                                                                     bi · · · ∧ xn
                                        i
                                            X
                                        +         (−1)i+j u ⊗ [xi , xj ] ∧ x1 ∧ · · · x
                                                                                      bi · · · x
                                                                                               b j · · · ∧ xn .
                                            i<j

In degree two this is ux ⊗ y − uy ⊗ x − u ⊗ [x, y]. Its next differential is u(xy − yx − [x, y]) = 0.
   In arbitrary degree, two module actions leave their commutator, which cancels the action of the
corresponding bracket. A bracket and an action on disjoint indices cancel in opposite orders. Two
bracket operations on three indices sum to Jacobi. These exhaust the terms of d2 . Filtering by
PBW degree plus exterior degree makes the associated graded the polynomial Koszul resolution.
This is a tensor product of one-variable exact complexes, and hence is exact; for infinitely many
variables use its filtered union. The increasing exhaustive-filtration lemma proves exactness of the
original resolution.

                                                           17
Chapter 5. Lie envelopes, parameters, and the diagonal


   For the Rees family over R = k[ℏ],

                                   Aℏ = TR (gR )/(xy − yx − ℏ[x, y]),

the bracket term in this resolution is multiplied by ℏ. It is the resolution for the R-Lie algebra with
bracket ℏ[−, −]. The PBW argument is unchanged. After applying the augmentation on the right,
only the ℏ-scaled bracket differential remains. Thus the augmentation bar is represented by

                                               Λ•R gR , ℏdCE .
                                                                 


A parameter-free Chevalley differential would compute a different family.


5.3     The coalgebra comparison
With the bar signs of Chapter 1, choose Chevalley chains with degree-two differential ∂(x∧y) = [x, y].
The antisymmetrization
                                                     X
                           an (x1 ∧ · · · ∧ xn ) =          sgn(σ)[xσ(1) | · · · |xσ(n) ]
                                                     σ∈Sn

is then a chain map to BU (g). Adjacent opposite permutations combine their products to a bracket;
the remaining letters keep exactly the exterior sign. In degree two the identity is b([x|y] − [y|x]) =
[[x, y]].
   It is a coalgebra map. A permutation with a cut after r letters is uniquely a choice of an r-element
subset, an order on that subset, and an order on its complement. The sign factors into the subset
shuffle and the two internal signs. This is the exterior coproduct. No division by n! is used.
    To prove it is a quasi-isomorphism, lift it to the exterior and normalized bar free resolutions of
the trivial module, adjusting the overall sign in each resolution degree if necessary. A comparison
lifting the identity between two projective resolutions is a homotopy equivalence: construct a reverse
map degree by degree by exactness and projectivity, then lift the differences of the composites from
the identity through the differentials to construct homotopies. Tensoring these homotopies with the
trivial module retains the identities. This proves the bar–Chevalley comparison with its coproduct.


5.4     Three Lie tests
For sl2 with [e, f ] = h, [h, e] = 2e, [h, f ] = −2f , the degree-two chain matrix in bases (e, f, h) and
(e ∧ f, e ∧ h, f ∧ h) is                                   
                                                  0 −2 0
                                                0 0 2 .
                                                           
                                                  1 0 0
It is invertible, and the degree-three differential is zero. Hence H• (BU (sl2 )) has Poincare polynomial
1 + t3 . This is not a planar path count.
  For the Heisenberg Lie algebra [x, y] = z, only ∂(x ∧ y) = z is nonzero. The positive homology
has bases
                     H1 : x, y;      H2 : x ∧ z, y ∧ z;     H3 : x ∧ y ∧ z.


                                                       18
Chapter 5. Lie envelopes, parameters, and the diagonal


Its bracket is visible in the canceled pair x ∧ y → z. Removing that pair as a complex does not give
a strict coalgebra splitting: the coproduct of x ∧ z contains x ⊗ z − z ⊗ x. A transferred higher
coalgebra retains the interaction.
   For the two-dimensional algebra [x, y] = y, the Rees augmentation complex is
                                                       −ℏy                0
                                 0 −→ R(x ∧ y) −−→ Rx ⊕ Ry −
                                                           → R −→ 0.
It has H0 = R, H1 = Rx ⊕ (R/ℏR)y, and H2 = 0. Its specialization at ℏ = 0 has zero differential
and nonzero degree-two homology. This class is produced by derived specialization of the degree-one
torsion. Underived specialization of the already computed H2 loses it.


5.5     The diagonal resolution is not the augmentation resolution
For a finite-dimensional g, the diagonal bimodule has a finite free resolution Aℏ ⊗ Λ• g ⊗ Aℏ , with
left action minus right action in the one-letter terms and the same ℏ-scaled bracket term. Filtering
by total PBW plus exterior degree gives the polynomial diagonal Koszul resolution on the regular
sequence xL      R
            i − xi . The filtration proof establishes exactness. Consequently the diagonal is perfect
even though Aℏ is usually infinite rank over R.
   For [x, y] = y, applying bimodule Hom gives
                                                          δ       δ
                                              Z:           0
                                                       Aℏ −→ A2ℏ −→
                                                                  1
                                                                    Aℏ ,
where
                       δ0 (f ) = ([x, f ], [y, f ]),      δ1 (P, Q) = [x, Q] − [y, P ] − ℏQ.
The composite is [[x, y], f ] − ℏ[y, f ] = 0. This is the actual relative diagonal-centre complex, not the
preceding augmentation complex.
Theorem 5.1 (Creation of central functions). For this family, H 0 (Z) = R, and there is a canonical
vector-space isomorphism
                                              ∼

Proof. Write f = b fb (x)y b in PBW form. Commutation with x forces b = 0, because [x, y b ] = ℏby b

and the PBW module is torsion-free. Then

5.6     Characters and the limits of a denominator
For a positively root-graded Lie superalgebra with finite pieces and finitely many decompositions of
each weight, the bar–Chevalley comparison gives the total signed Euler character

                                                (1 − X γ )dim nγ,0 −dim nγ,1 .
                                            Y
                              χΓ BU (n) =
                                            γ>0

An even Lie generator suspends to an odd exterior generator, contributing 1 − X γ ; an odd one
suspends to a total-even symmetric generator, contributing its inverse. Finiteness in each weight
makes the multiplication and Euler calculation well defined. Using only homological signs instead of
total parity changes the odd-root factor.
   This is an Euler identity, not a reconstruction of the bracket or of an automorphic form. The
abelian and Heisenberg brackets on vector spaces with generator weights a, b, a + b have the same
PBW character and different differentials. A denominator comparison needs an actual bracket-
preserving map, root multiplicities and normalization before it can prove an identification. The
positive Witt weights and the previously corrected sl3 and G2 Poincare polynomials remain separate
Lie-homology inputs; no Motzkin or Riordan enumeration is substituted for them.




                                                   20
Chapter 6

Modules, quadratic comparisons, and
the category a probe reconstructs

6.1     A module can replace a missing scalar augmentation
An augmented algebra has two distinguished scalar endpoint modules. A unital algebra need not
have either. For the Weyl algebra pq − qp = 1, a scalar augmentation would send the left side to
zero and the right side to one. For a matrix algebra of rank greater than one, a unital scalar map
would annihilate its commutators and contradict simplicity. Neither algebra is thereby excluded
from a reconstruction theory. What has to change is the probe, not the multiplication.
   For a unital dg algebra A and a left dg module M , the simplicial free module resolution has
terms
                                      Pn = A ⊗ A⊗n ⊗ M.
Its faces multiply neighbouring algebra factors and, at the last face, apply the action on M . Its
degeneracies insert the unit. Associativity and the module axiom give all simplicial identities. The
augmentation applies the iterated action. Before normalization it has an extra degeneracy inserting
the unit to the left; the alternating sum of faces composed with that degeneracy is the identity
minus the augmentation section.
Theorem 6.1 (The module bar resolution). The normalized realization of P• is a free graded
A-module resolution of M , with derived tensors understood when required. It exists for every unital
dg algebra over a field, without a scalar augmentation. For a right module N , its tensor with N
computes N ⊗LA M .

Proof. Use internal degree q and simplicial degree n, with total degree q − n. The total differential
is dint + (−1)q i (−1)i di . The simplicial identities pair every twice-applied face with the same
                 P

composite carrying the opposite sign. The internal differential commutes with every face before
totalization, so the total mixed terms cancel. The extra degeneracy gives the asserted augmentation
contraction as a complex of vector spaces. The subcomplex generated by degenerate simplices
is contracted by the usual first-unit insertion, or by filtering a degenerate word by the first slot
occupied by an inserted unit and pairing its two faces. Hence the normalized quotient has the same
cohomology as the original realization and resolves M .
   Each normalized term is a free A-module on a complex of vector spaces. Choosing splittings of
its underlying complex into boundaries, representatives and complements gives a homotopically

                                                 21
Chapter 6. Modules, quadratic comparisons, and the category a probe reconstructs


projective resolution, or equivalently one may retain the simplicial free resolution as the definition
of the derived realization. Tensoring that free resolution with N is precisely the two-sided bar. It
therefore computes the derived tensor product.

   The quotient A/k1 in this normalization is not an augmentation ideal and is not given a new
multiplication. Interior and endpoint faces descend together from the simplicial object. Thus this
theorem must not be rewritten as an augmented tensor coalgebra construction for a Weyl algebra.


6.2     A quadratic complex has to be proved exact
Let A = T (V )/(R2 ) be a connected quadratic algebra. Put
                                     n−2
                                           V ⊗i ⊗ R2 ⊗ V ⊗(n−i−2) ⊆ V ⊗n ,
                                     \
                              Kn =
                                     i=0

with K0 = k and K1 = V . The left quadratic complex is A ⊗ Kn , with differential multiplying the
first letter. The inclusion Kn ⊆ R2 ⊗ V ⊗n−2 makes its square zero. Deconcatenation preserves the
corresponding quadratic coalgebra.

Proposition 6.2 (The precise quadratic comparison). If A ⊗ K• resolves the augmentation module,
its antisuspended inclusion into the normalized module bar is a quasi-isomorphism. The induced
coalgebra map K → BA is a quasi-isomorphism. Conversely, this bar comparison implies exactness
of the quadratic resolution. A quadratic presentation alone supplies the complex, not its exactness.

Proof. In Kn every internal multiplication lands in a defining relation, so the bar differential has
exactly the action component of the quadratic differential. Thus inclusion is a chain map. If both
augmented complexes resolve k, lift the identity recursively through their projective terms to get
a reverse comparison and chain homotopies; in degree −n exactness supplies the cycle to lift and
projectivity supplies its lift. Tensoring the homotopies with k proves the coalgebra comparison on
underlying complexes. Deconcatenation compatibility follows from its definition on the intersection
spaces. Conversely, applying the same twisted free-module construction to a bar quasi-isomorphism
in the connected weight category gives the module comparison; the finite-weight filtration has tensor
powers on its associated graded. Since the free bar resolves k, so does the quadratic complex.

   For a polynomial algebra on finitely many even variables, K is the exterior coalgebra. The
                                                               x
resolution is the tensor product of the exact complexes [k[x] −
                                                              → k[x]] and is exact away from the
augmentation. For a free algebra, the only positive term is A ⊗ V because every nonempty word
has a unique last letter. For the quantum and Jordan planes, the two-generator PBW reductions of
Chapter 4 give the displayed length-two resolution. Each example verifies exactness in its own way;
no character identity is used as a substitute for it.


6.3     A perfect probe reconstructs its thick category
Let P be a perfect left A-module and set E = RHomA (P, P ). Regard the Hom into another module
as a right E-module by precomposition. Derived evaluation gives the adjunction

                            − ⊗LE P : ModE op ⇄ ModA : RHomA (P, −).

                                                    22
Chapter 6. Modules, quadratic comparisons, and the category a probe reconstructs


A right module notation may equivalently be used in place of ModE op ; the variance is part of the
assertion.

Theorem 6.3 (Probe Morita reconstruction). The adjunction restricts to inverse equivalences

                                       Perf(E op ) ≃ thickA (P ).

If P is also a compact generator of the whole enhanced derived category, the equivalence extends to
that whole category and all E-modules.

Proof. The unit is the identity on the free right E-module and the counit is the evaluation equivalence
on P . The objects for which either comparison is an equivalence are closed under shifts, finite
cones and retracts. The first classes are therefore all perfect modules on one side and the thick
category generated by P on the other. This proves the first claim, including full faithfulness on all
mapping complexes. For the second, compactness makes the Hom functor preserve coproducts. The
equivalence loci of unit and counit are then localizing subcategories; generation by E and P makes
them the whole categories.

   Faithfulness as a representation does not imply this generating hypothesis. The polynomial
Weyl vacuum is faithful in characteristic zero, but the nonzero module on which its annihilator p
acts invertibly has zero derived Hom from that vacuum. Chapter 24 constructs the resolution and
computes that example. A Morita theorem on the category it generates remains true; enlarging its
conclusion to all modules does not.


6.4     The regular diagonal and its centre
The regular A–A bimodule is the unit for derived composition of bimodules. Its endomorphism
complex is
                                Z(A) = RHomA⊗Aop (A, A).
The composition of natural transformations and the composition of bimodules supply the two
coherent compositions behind the ordinary E2 structure. The ordinary Deligne theorem identifies
this structure with the brace construction. That topological identification is a named classical input
[5, 6, 11]; the cochain formulas are written explicitly in Chapter 18.
   A Morita equivalence implemented by mutually inverse bimodules sends the regular diagonal to
the regular diagonal. It therefore preserves the centre, with those coherent compositions. Merely
choosing a faithful module, or merely producing an algebra map, does not have that effect. In
particular an algebra quotient does not functorially send every adjoint Hochschild cochain to the
quotient: ∂x of k[x] does not descend through x = 0.

Koszul–Morita square

7.1     The objects and the two twists
Let A = k ⊕ w>0 Aw be an augmented dg algebra, with differential preserving weight and
               L

multiplication adding it. Let C = BA and let τ : C → A be the degree-one twisting cochain, equal
to sa 7→ a on one-letter bars and zero otherwise. The equation

                                    dA τ + τ dC + µ(τ ⊗ τ )∆C = 0

uses the Koszul sign in applying τ ⊗ τ . For two letters its last two terms have coefficients (−1)|a|
and (−1)|a|−1 .
   We use modules and conilpotent comodules whose auxiliary weights have a fixed lower bound.
Maps preserve weight; homogeneous shifted maps will be considered at the end. The coaction on a
comodule N has terms c ⊗ n′ with c of nonnegative weight, and its reduced part strictly lowers the
weight of n′ . It is therefore finite on every homogeneous element.
   For a left A-module M , define
                                           K(M ) = C ⊗ M,
with its cofree left coaction. Its differential is the sum of the tensor internal differential and the
last-slot twist
                                               (−1)|c(1) | c(1) ⊗ τ (c(2) )m.
                                           X
                                c ⊗ m 7−→
For a left C-comodule N , define L(N ) = A ⊗ N , with free left action and

                   dL (a ⊗ n) = dA a ⊗ n + (−1)|a| a ⊗ dN n − (−1)|a|       aτ (c) ⊗ n′ .
                                                                        X
                                                                                                   (7.1)

The final minus sign is not optional. It distinguishes the first-action twist from the last-action twist
in this suspension convention.
   Both squares vanish. For K, disjoint operations cancel by the tensor signs; the two operations
meeting the last slot cancel by the module axiom and the two-letter twisting equation. For L, on
the free generator 1 ⊗ n the internal–twist terms give minus dA τ + τ dC , while two twists give minus
µ(τ ⊗ τ )∆C . Coassociativity identifies all the terms. Their sum is zero. The graded Leibniz rule
extends the calculation to every a ⊗ n.
